`tests / Script Tests (Linux)` in the develop "Validate develop" workflow has been red since 2026-09-11. One of its causes is `packages/scripts/__tests__/staging-cloud-receipt.test.ts`: two cases fail before any receipt assertion runs.

```
error: [cloud-live-continuity] artifact must use the exact closed schema
      at requireClosedRecord (packages/app/test/cloud-live-continuity-contract.ts:2134:5)
      at parseCloudLiveContinuityEvidence (packages/app/test/cloud-live-continuity-contract.ts:2379:20)
      at createDeployedRendererProof (packages/cloud/scripts/pages-deployment-authority.mjs:836:22)
      at deployedProofFile (packages/scripts/__tests__/staging-cloud-receipt.test.ts:72:17)
(fail) staging Cloud live receipt > persists closed correlation in receipt v4 from a validated proof file
(fail) staging Cloud live receipt > rejects boolean claims and proof/source/latency mismatches
```

Latest red run: https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/34723784763/job/103635168326 (develop `1fa9dbf22d74`).

## Root cause

`7e9e3f946a0e` ("fix(cloud): validate deployed continuity with the browser contract") made `createDeployedRendererProof` parse its `continuity` input with `parseCloudLiveContinuityEvidence`, which requires the closed `schemaVersion: 2` record including the Dedicated mutation keys. The sibling suite `packages/cloud/scripts/__tests__/pages-deployment-authority.test.ts` was updated in that commit to build the record with `createCloudLiveContinuityEvidence`; this test was not.

## Acceptance criteria

- `bun test packages/scripts/__tests__/staging-cloud-receipt.test.ts` passes (8/8) on develop.
- The proof fixture is produced by the continuity contract's own builder rather than a hand-copied literal, so future key additions cannot silently drift.
- The "does not match the receipt source/run identity" and "latency does not match" rejections are still exercised.

d:T78e,## Reproduced failure

The deployed Bettina pilot at `40bf83d22b150d71632878b3868097cc51ccd713` now discovers and invokes the requested household action. A real owner request for `HOUSEHOLD_COORDINATION_ISSUE_GRANT` supplied `subjectEntityIds: []` with `scopes: ["knowledge.read"]`, a persisted synthetic caregiver role and an explicit household/principal. The action returned `MISSING_HOUSEHOLD_PARAMETERS` naming `subjectEntityIds` even though that parameter was supplied. A subsequent owner export independently confirmed that no household grant was stored for the synthetic principal.

The generic `resolveActionArgs` presence check treats every empty required array as missing. `HouseholdCoordinationService.issueGrant` deliberately permits an empty subject list for a knowledge-only grant; it separately rejects non-owner calendar grants without subjects. The action resolver prevents this valid domain contract from reaching the service.

## Acceptance

- Preserve the distinction between an omitted parameter and an explicitly supplied empty array for action parameters whose domain allows empty lists.
- Allow the household knowledge-only grant with explicit `subjectEntityIds: []` through the real owner action path and persist the actual grant.
- Keep omitted subjects, malformed arrays, unauthorized principals/roles, out-of-relationship subjects and non-owner calendar grants without subjects rejected by their existing boundaries. Do not make all required arrays optional or silently default an omitted array to empty.
- Cover the resolver and promoted household action with meaningful regression tests, run owning package/root and hosted checks, and complete the paired-guest read/revoke/expiry/export acceptance on the deployed pilot.

Part of #30123, #31172 and #31162. The action-discovery repair #31181 is separately verified: it successfully executes the role binding and exposes this downstream parameter defect.
e:T639,## Defect

`packages/scripts/audit-tsconfig-workspace-resolution.test.mjs` case 6, "historic app and Electrobun mappings are real red-green controls", is red on `develop` (tip `ce68dbca425412131bd2d042b2cc4ffa6c8a61f7`; `tests / Script Tests (Linux)` and `canonical / Tests (scripts)` in run 34694307489):

```text
node --test packages/scripts/audit-tsconfig-workspace-resolution.test.mjs
not ok 6 - historic app and Electrobun mappings are real red-green controls
  The input did not match the regular expression /platforms\/electrobun\/tsconfig\.json: unresolved @elizaos\/capacitor-bun-runtime imported by packages\/app-core\/src\/platform\/ios-runtime-bridge\.ts/. Input:
  '@elizaos/electrobun typecheck -> packages/app-core/platforms/electrobun/tsconfig.json: unresolved @elizaos/capacitor-bun-runtime imported by packages/ui/src/api/ios-local-agent-transport.ts'
# pass 5
# fail 1
```

## Cause

The control removes the `@elizaos/capacitor-bun-runtime` path mapping from the Electrobun tsconfig and expects the audit to report it unresolved. It also pins the *importer* to `packages/app-core/src/platform/ios-runtime-bridge.ts`. The audit reports the first importer it meets, and since `c745213d6100` (fix: consolidate duplicate systems behind canonical owners, #30229) that is `packages/ui/src/api/ios-local-agent-transport.ts`. The same pin already broke once and was re-pointed in #30709.

## Fix

Match any workspace importer (`imported by packages/…`). The contract the control proves is that the missing mapping is detected, not which file imports the package first. Test-only.

13:T81b,## Summary

plugin-computeruse's OCR / Set-of-Marks provider registry is bundled twice, so plugin-vision's registration never reaches the readers in packaged builds. `plugins/plugin-computeruse/build.ts:52` builds `src/mobile/ocr-provider.ts` as a standalone entrypoint (`dist/mobile/ocr-provider.js`) while `src/index.ts` also inlines the same module into `dist/index.js` (there the writer is tree-shaken and only `var registeredCoordOcrProvider = null` plus the getter survive). plugin-vision writes through `await import("@elizaos/plugin-computeruse/mobile/ocr-provider")` (`plugins/plugin-vision/src/index.ts:118-141`), which resolves to the subpath bundle; the readers `computer-use-service.ts:1231` (`runOcrOrDetect`), `scene/ocr-adapter.ts:98,156` and `app-control/defaults.ts:88,114` live in `dist/index.js` and read the other module instance.

## Reproduction (develop `5b183d21ff25`, built dist)

```
$ grep -c 'function registerCoordOcrProvider' dist/index.js dist/mobile/ocr-provider.js
dist/index.js:0
dist/mobile/ocr-provider.js:1
```

A probe importing both bundles without `--conditions`, registering through the subpath and listing through the index: `subpath listOcrProviders -> ["probe-ocr"]` / `index listOcrProviders -> []`.

## Consequence

In packaged desktop or npm-consumed builds `ocr` and `detect_elements` always fail with "No OCR provider is registered. Enable @elizaos/plugin-vision…" and `scene.ocr` stays empty even with plugin-vision loaded, while vision logs "registered coord-OCR bridge". Every repo dev/start script passes `--conditions=eliza-source` (both imports resolve to `src/`), which masks it. `plugins/plugin-computeruse/CLAUDE.md:275` documents this registry as the extension path.

## Acceptance criteria

14:T8b6,## Summary

The auth audit log writer `appendJsonLine` (`packages/app-core/src/api/auth/audit.ts:115-121`) does `fs.appendFile(filePath, JSON.stringify(line) + "\n")` into `<state>/auth/audit.log`, the file the header documents as the operator's history "even if pglite is wiped". A crash between the write and the newline glues the next event onto the torn line, and any line-oriented reader skips both. This is the same shape #30878 describes; #30879 adds `appendJsonlRecord` to `@elizaos/shared` but fixes only `capability-broker.ts`, `account-pool-consumer-metering.ts` and `account-usage.ts`.

## Reproduction (develop `5b183d21ff25`)

Probe calling the exported `appendAuditEvent` with a stub store and `ELIZA_STATE_DIR` at a temp dir, torn bytes appended between two real events:

```
LINES: 2 [ "login", "<unparseable: {\"id\":\"torn\",\"ts\":2,\"actorIdentityId\":\"id-1\",\"ip\":\"127.0.0.1...>" ]
```

The second real event (`token_revoke`) is inside the unparseable line and invisible to `jq -c`, `grep`, or the rotate-then-read workflow the header describes.

## Other writers with the same shape (no in-repo reader, ranked by data value)

`packages/vault/src/audit.ts:26-29`, `plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator/src/services/audit.ts:70-76` (`audit.ndjson`), `plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator/src/services/orchestrator-task-service.ts:3025-3034` (completion evidence appended to a trajectory file), `plugins/plugin-app-control/src/services/app-registry-service.ts:257-262`, `plugins/plugin-agent-orchestrator/src/services/subagent-stdout-log.ts:75`, `packages/app-core/platforms/electrobun/src/startup-trace.ts:101-103`, `plugins/plugin-capacitor-bridge/src/android/bridge.ts:387`. `packages/corpus-tools/src/collectors/gmail.ts:1297` is the same shape but its reader re-fetches dropped rows, so it is benign. `packages/app-core/src/services/account-pool-status.ts:347` loses one diagnostic snapshot after a torn tail (`readSnapshots` skips the glued line).

## Acceptance criteria

- Once #30879 lands, the auth audit writer (and the listed siblings) append through `appendJsonlRecord` so a torn line isolates only itself.
- A test appends torn bytes between two events and asserts the second event stays readable.
15:T706,## Summary

Meeting-ghost relative due dates are resolved on the UTC calendar day of the meeting. `parseDueDate` in `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/lifeops/meeting-ghost/index.ts:355-370` does `new Date(meetingStartedAt)`, adds days with `setUTCDate`, reads the weekday with `getUTCDay()` and formats with `toISOString().slice(0,10)`; `dueDateToLedgerDueAt` (373-377) then pins `${dueDate}T17:00:00.000Z` and the calendar deadline (436) `T09:00:00.000Z`. `event-handler.ts:67` feeds every transcript through this path.

## Reproduction (develop `5b183d21ff25`)

Probe calling the exported `analyzeMeetingGhostTranscript` (results are offset-driven, identical under any process TZ):

```
startedAt=2026-09-14T17:30:00-07:00 "Alice will send the report by tomorrow" -> dueDate=2026-09-16  (Mon 5:30pm PT; tomorrow is 09-15)
startedAt=2026-09-17T17:30:00-07:00 "Bob will send the deck by Friday"       -> dueDate=2026-09-25  (Thu 5:30pm PT; Friday is 09-18)
startedAt=2026-09-14T08:00:00+09:00 "Alice will send the report by tomorrow" -> dueDate=2026-09-14  (Mon 8am JST; tomorrow is 09-15)
startedAt=2026-09-14T08:00:00+09:00 "Carol will file the taxes by Monday"    -> dueDate=2026-09-14  (said on Monday; next Monday is 09-21)
```

## Consequence

The commitment ledger `dueAt`, the follow-up approvals and the "Deadline:" calendar intent carry the wrong day, a full week off in the weekday case, for any meeting whose local evening is already the next UTC day (afternoon/evening in the Americas) or whose local morning is still the previous UTC day (east of UTC).

## Acceptance criteria

- Relative dates are resolved on the meeting's local calendar day (the transcript's or runtime's timezone), and the ledger/calendar instants are built from that zone.
- Tests pin the four cases above.

19:T68b,## Summary

`packages/cloud/services/gateway-discord/src/server-router.ts:26` and `packages/cloud/services/gateway-webhook/src/server-router.ts:21` compute `Number(process.env.KEDA_COOLDOWN_SECONDS ?? 900)`. `??` guards only an unset variable, so any present-but-invalid value yields `NaN`, which flows into `redis.expire(key, NaN)` in `_common/src/gateway-routing.ts:40`. The `@upstash/redis` client drops the `NaN` argument and sends a one-argument `EXPIRE`, which Redis rejects.

## Reproduction (develop `5b183d21ff25`)

`bun --conditions=eliza-source test` probe in gateway-discord with `KEDA_COOLDOWN_SECONDS=abc`, fetch overridden to capture the Upstash pipeline body:

```

PROBE thrown: Command failed: ERR value is not an integer or out of range
```

## Consequence

In gateway-discord the rejection lands in the `catch` at `gateway-manager.ts:1650` that also wraps `forwardToServer`, so every inbound message is counted as failed and never forwarded (`Failed to route message to server`). gateway-webhook only warns, but `lpush`/`ltrim` already ran, so the activity key persists with no TTL and KEDA never scales the server down. #30544 fixed the `parseInt(env || d)` shape in cloud/shared and states `Number(...)` is safe; that holds for `Number(x) || d`, not `Number(x ?? d)`.

## Acceptance criteria

- An invalid `KEDA_COOLDOWN_SECONDS` fails fast at boot with a typed error naming the variable (or falls back to the default with a warning; pick one and document it), never `NaN`.
- Tests pin the invalid-value case for both routers.

23:T8ba,## Summary

With the home-screen step green again (#31199), `ui-core / ui-core-fixture-e2e` on develop now fails at its next step, "Frontend hosting e2e" (`bun run --cwd packages/ui test:frontend-hosting-e2e`):

```
✗ primary Publish button is orange-accent, not blue/black (0,0,0)
✗ hover is a DARKER orange — changed from rest, never orange→black/blue (rest 0,0,0 → hover 38,38,38)
✗ 2 assertion(s) failed
```

Latest red run: https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/34754721691/job/103717233231 (develop `5b183d21ff25`).

## Root cause

`run-frontend-hosting-e2e.mjs` compiles the real Tailwind theme (`styles/base.css` + `styles/tailwind-theme.css`) and reads the computed background of `[data-testid="hosting-publish"]`, a default-variant `Button` (`bg-accent … hover:bg-accent-hover`). Since `d274fc06b832` ("fix(ui): unify grey surfaces and simplify view presentation", 2026-09-05, part of the same design pass as #31214) `base.css` defines the light-theme accent as black:

```css
--accent: var(--brand-black);
--accent-rgb: 0, 0, 0;
--accent-hover: #262626;
```

so the button renders `rgb(0,0,0)` at rest and `rgb(38,38,38)` on hover, exactly what the runner reports. The orange accent survives only in `styles/brand-gold.css` (`--accent: var(--classic-gold)`), which this e2e does not load. The repository guide still states the visual contract the e2e enforces (`CLAUDE.md`: "Orange is the accent; do not introduce blue, and use darker orange—not black—for an orange resting control's hover state").

## What needs deciding

## Root cause

`.github/workflows/test.yml` runs

```
bun run --cwd packages/app-core test -- test/app/screenshot-quality.test.ts
```

but `packages/app-core/package.json#test` is a `&&` chain (`node ../scripts/run-vitest.mjs run --config vitest.config.ts && bun run test:script-suites`). Bun appends the extra argument to the script text, so it lands on the **last** command; locally the expansion is visible on the first output line:

```
$ node ../scripts/run-vitest.mjs run --config vitest.config.ts && bun run test:script-suites test/app/screenshot-quality.test.ts
```

vitest therefore runs unfiltered. Since `6dbff6e528b8` (2026-09-10) the app-core suite includes `steward-sidecar-login.test.ts`, which spawns the bundled login child and requires the `@elizaos/login` workspace (or `STEWARD_ENTRY_POINT`); the diagnostics job builds only `packages/cloud/routing`, so that test fails and takes the lane with it. The neighbouring "App screenshot quality diagnostics coverage" step already avoids this by invoking `bunx vitest run --config … <file>` directly.

# Risks

**High, contained by the hard-off gate.** Two reasons: the repository gains a new
schema (two migrations, six tables and six guard triggers), and the two active
production container-create paths change how they reserve node capacity.

The hard-off gate contains only the automatic failover path. It does not disable
ordinary container creation, which is exactly where this PR changes existing
behaviour, so that part must be reviewed on its own merits rather than assumed
inert.

- The migrations are additive and unregistered by anything: no route, worker, or
  cron reads the six new tables.
- `DockerSandboxProvider` and `HetznerContainersClient` now reserve a slot with a
  compare-and-set bound to the exact node occurrence instead of a blind increment
  by node handle. This is the only behaviour change in existing production code:
  a node cordoned, replaced, re-attested, or filled between selection and
  reservation is now refused rather than used.
- Rollback now releases the same occurrence it reserved; a refused release is
  logged as needing a recount instead of silently decrementing a same-named node.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Adds the durable foundation for recovering Agents when a node occurrence stops
answering:

- **Fault records and evidence** — `agent_node_failure_events`, keyed to one
  immutable node occurrence, plus append-only `agent_node_failure_signals`. The
  verdict is `triggered`, not `confirmed`: corroboration starts recovery work, it
  does not establish that a node is dead.
- **Declared independence** — observers are a controlled enumeration, each signal
  records the independence class and policy version it was collected under, and a
  database constraint pins the source-to-class mapping. Two probes down one
  network path cannot corroborate each other.
- **Frozen fault scene** — `agent_node_failure_candidates` is written inside the
  transaction that cordons the node, from the activation publications that
  actually authorize each Agent on that occurrence.
- **Processing state and concurrency** — `agent_failover_operations` (executor
  lease with a rotating generation and DB-clock horizon, frozen source authority,
  referenced restore authority, cutover identity bundle) and
  `agent_failover_step_attempts` (append-only progress journal).
- **Guards** — steps advance one at a time, an interrupted attempt must be
  reconciled before its step runs again, a superseded executor cannot settle
  anything, and a committed cutover cannot be cancelled or failed.

`packages/cloud/shared/docs/agent-failover-contract.md` states what is proven and
what is explicitly deferred.

## What kind of change is this?

Feature (non-breaking; the new surface is unused and the failover switch is hard
off).

## Why are we doing this?

Moving an Agent between nodes must not produce two live instances, a blank Agent
presented as recovered, or two copies of one recovery. Those failures are worse
than downtime, so the durable state machine and its guards land first and
separately, with the automated behaviour behind a switch that cannot be turned on.

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes require a change to the project documentation: this PR adds
`packages/cloud/shared/docs/agent-failover-contract.md`, which records the
contracts, the proof boundary, and the open decisions.

`packages/cloud/shared/src/db/migrations/0387_agent_failover_guards.sql` for what
the database refuses.

   still throws in

3. **Deferred to the wiring and enablement phase, not claimed here:**

     state write, outbound send). Recording an authority chain is not isolation;
     nothing in this PR rejects a stale instance at a boundary.

     cleanup of the old container.

2c:T2ec4,# Relates to

Part of #31180 and #30123. This PR adds the storage and admission foundation for reviewed family deletion; it does not close either issue.

# Background

Owner-bound dependency snapshots now fingerprint complete persisted rows while exposing only reviewable identities. The guarded transaction rejects stale previews and unsettled operations. Agreement uploads, review decisions, pins, grants, warning intents, and scheduled family mutations participate in the durable lifecycle fence.

Scheduled ownership includes monthly tasks, grant warnings, and approval reminders, including a canonical household approval whose domain link has not been acknowledged. Removing task metadata cannot bypass the fence. Execution admission and the scheduler's metadata compare-and-swap keep the observed task associated with the execution, and the operation stays unsettled until final receipt persistence. Lost source-write acknowledgements preserve reconciliation identity instead of reporting successful rollback without proof.

# Risks

Medium: short database transactions acquire ordered table locks around admission. Unknown write or execution outcomes retain durable claims and therefore block deletion; elapsed time does not clear them. Typed domain errors remain intact for callers, with reconciliation identity recorded in diagnostics. Unrelated tasks and approvals remain usable.

The complete owner deletion UI/API, primary/derived/cache purge, backup retention, remaining packet/school writers, and deployed acceptance remain in #31180. This PR neither invokes deletion on the pilot nor changes provider events or messages.

Rebased without conflicts on `b0e467c7ddef98951a234f1765ab1a32e434c043`; `bun install` and all checks below ran afterward at `e3c2d5db8efbba3ae126bf4d6b09ef688136f04d`.

- `bun run verify`: 373/373 tasks, all repository audits passed.
- Personal-assistant package tests: 2,662 passed, six existing skips, 303 files.
- Scheduling package tests: 642 passed, 36 files.
- Four real-PGlite agreement, household, and scheduled-store suites: 71 passed.
- Both packages' typecheck and lint:check passed.
- Independent artifact capture: two real-runtime database cases passed; captured JSON rows were manually inspected and their checksums reverified after public download.

The document, metadata-race, unlinked-warning, and missing-approval-reminder regressions were observed failing before repair. The resulting records show preserved family task/history rows, an unrelated approval becoming dismissed after fencing, and unrelated task/history deletion remaining atomic under an injected SQL failure.

30:T6d7,## Summary

Addresses #31070.

In paired remote sessions, bearer tokens and non-default API base URLs are managed by the canonical ElizaClient transport (`client`). Previously, FamilyOperationsAdapter issued raw fetch calls directly to `/api/...`, bypassing `client.fetch`. Consequently:
1. Requests lacked Authorization headers, triggering HTTP 401 Unauthorized errors in paired remote sessions.
2. In non-default host environments, bare path requests failed to route to the bound remote agent URL (`client.baseUrl`).

## Changes

- Defined `FamilyOperationsApiClient = Pick<ElizaClient, "fetch">`.
- Refactored `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/components/family-operations/adapter.ts` to export `createFamilyOperationsAdapter(apiClient: FamilyOperationsApiClient = client)` and bound `defaultFamilyOperationsAdapter = createFamilyOperationsAdapter(client)`.
- Delegated all queries (agreements, calendar links, school status, packets, email options), binary agreement PDF chunk uploads, and workflow mutations through `apiClient.fetch`.
- Updated binary chunk upload headers to explicitly pass `Content-Type: application/octet-stream` alongside `x-chunk-sha256`.
- Updated test stub `ElizaClient` in `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/test/stubs/ui.ts` to implement `fetch`.
- Added test coverage in `adapter.test.ts` verifying that `createFamilyOperationsAdapter` routes queries, binary chunk uploads, and mutations through the injected `apiClient`.

## Testing

- Added unit tests in `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/components/family-operations/adapter.test.ts` exercising `createFamilyOperationsAdapter` against a mock client double, validating that all paths, methods, and chunk binary upload headers pass through `apiClient.fetch`.31:T8f2,When a Dedicated agent cannot pay its accrued compute, billing currently gives it 48 hours of grace and depends on warning-email delivery before scheduling a stop. A separate five-second creation timestamp assumption also leaves one live staging agent permanently failing billing.

This eight-file promotion queues an immediate, durable billing stop without an email dependency and correctly handles the explicit revision-zero, zero-rate creation interval. It preserves funding rechecks, retry receipts, workload-generation fencing and rejection of incomplete rate history. A top-up before provider stop still wins the locked settlement recheck.

Source PRs #31284 and #31287 are merged. Production head `a8de106ae64f8e895b1ec00553e1d34c93ee2e65` has whole tree `de5c544df96c27075649987d81efbe2756fe4ede`, exactly matching staging candidate `48114e60dc50a3bb312a229ce7b3e7ef2bf53dd9`. No broad develop merge, rate change, existing-balance reset, schema migration or runtime image change.

Validation: all 373 source repository verification tasks and final audits passed. Combined staging runs passed 83 database/cron tests (602 assertions) and API TypeScript/router/production Worker bundle checks. The two slow-creation regressions failed before the fix. All five hosted production checks passed. Canonical staging Cloud release 34780536173 and control-plane release 34781409370 succeeded at 5e308dab. The live staging zero-balance test admitted a durable billing stop, created a fresh backup, and confirmed provider shutdown in about 45 seconds. The temporary hold of existing founder credits was fully refunded; no card charge or grant was made. Backup decryption and manifest verification passed (content hash was not checked). After funded recovery through the already-open app, all 49 rendered messages matched the pre-stop history exactly after reload. The test used the authorized deployed service on the control plane; it was not a public HTTP cron test.

A queued stop does not prove provider shutdown. Prepaid hosting reservations, provider paid-time expiry, cost-based pricing/capacity and storage expiry remain outstanding under #31280. Automatic VM expansion is separately paused in staging and production. UI screenshots: N/A for these backend billing changes.

Refs #31280.
32:T5a0,Local `bun run dev` users who completed Cloud sign-in could loop back through login on `/cloud/billing`, then send account requests to the local agent and receive `Not found`. Claim the one-time callback on the app settings route before returning to the requested account page, verify the CLI credential against the staging account authority, and route account requests to that same authority.

This behavior is confined to the explicitly configured loopback staging development lane. Offline cookie-backed local Cloud keeps its same-origin authority until a CLI credential is claimed. Hosted and native authentication, cross-origin restrictions, and the server requirement for interactive billing mutations remain intact. Invalid credentials and stale responses cannot authenticate a different account.

Validation: 91 focused callback, provider, and transport tests passed; UI typecheck, nine-file Biome, and diff checks passed. Root verification completed 373 tasks and repository audits; the later transport/provider changes were covered by the final focused tests and UI typecheck. The real local Billing payment replay, including agent-status503 reload, passed after the offline-cookie routing regression was corrected. Native Chrome testing with the owner profile completed normal Cloud login, restored the existing 41-message conversation, and displayed the correct Billing account, balance, and Dedicated resource.

Closes #31207.
33:Ted2,Closes #31245

## What

- `packages/cloud/services/_common/src/integer-env.ts` (new, exported as `./integer-env`): the lexical integer parser previously private to gateway-discord, plus `parsePositiveIntegerEnvValue(name, value, default)` which applies the default only when the variable is unset.
- `packages/cloud/services/_common/src/gateway-routing.ts`: `resolveKedaCooldownSeconds(value)` (default 900) next to the `refreshGatewayActivity` it feeds.
- `gateway-discord/src/server-router.ts` and `gateway-webhook/src/server-router.ts` call `resolveKedaCooldownSeconds(process.env.KEDA_COOLDOWN_SECONDS)` at module load.
- `gateway-discord/src/integer-env.ts` becomes a re-export of the shared parser (existing importers and `integer-env-parsing.test.ts` unchanged).
- New tests: `_common/tests/integer-env.test.ts`, `gateway-discord/tests/keda-cooldown-env.test.ts`, `gateway-webhook/__tests__/keda-cooldown-env.test.ts`. Env docs updated in the three CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md pairs.

## Why

`Number(process.env.KEDA_COOLDOWN_SECONDS ?? 900)` guards only an unset variable, so a present-but-invalid value yields `NaN` (or `0` for an empty string, or a negative TTL), which reaches `redis.expire(key, NaN)` in `_common/src/gateway-routing.ts`. The Upstash client drops the `NaN` argument and Redis rejects the one-argument `EXPIRE`; in gateway-discord that rejection lands in the same `catch` that wraps `forwardToServer`, so every inbound message is counted as failed and never forwarded, and in gateway-webhook `lpush`/`ltrim` have already run so the activity key persists with no TTL and KEDA never scales down. The fix follows the existing gateway-discord policy for numeric env (`parseIntegerEnvValue` + fatal `ElizaError` `INVALID_GATEWAY_INTEGER_ENV`): the value must be a positive safe integer or the router module refuses to load, so a misconfiguration stops boot with an error naming `KEDA_COOLDOWN_SECONDS` rather than breaking routing at the first message.

35:T79f,When a Dedicated agent cannot pay accrued compute, the billing cron currently waits for a warning email to be accepted and then allows another 48 hours before requesting suspension. Email failure can prevent the stop from being armed.

This change rechecks settlement under the billing-run lease and lifecycle lock, then atomically commits the pending state, durable suspend job, and billing receipt. Stops are due immediately. 38:T12cb,Closes #31240

## What

In `packages/agent/src/api/remote-capability-routes.ts`:

- `readPersistedEndpoints`, `readPersistedModuleAllowlists`, `readPersistedTrustPolicies`, and `readTrustAuditRecords` no longer turn an unparseable stored value into `[]` / `{}`. A shared `parsePersistedJson` throws a typed `ElizaError` with code `CAPABILITY_ROUTER_PERSISTED_SETTING_INVALID`, context `{ setting, reason }`, and the `JSON.parse` cause (error-policy J3); a wrong top-level JSON type (object where an array is expected, or vice versa) is rejected the same way. A malformed trust-policy entry is rewrapped as the invalid setting it lives in (J2) instead of being swallowed into `{}`. The trust-policy reader was not named in the issue but had the identical `catch { return {} }` shape, so it is fixed alongside the three named readers.
- The connect route reads all four settings through `loadPersistedCapabilityRouterState` **before** it calls the endpoint or cloud provider, and `persistEndpoint` consumes that snapshot. A corrupt setting therefore fails the request with a structured 500 naming the setting, the runtime is not mutated, `persistConfigEnv`/`saveConfig` are never called, and the stored value is left untouched. The route catch (J1) answers 500 for this error class and keeps 400 for request-validation and provider failures. The pre-existing "persistence is unavailable in this runtime" check also moves ahead of the connect as part of the same fail-closed ordering. `persist: false` connects are unaffected.

Tests in `remote-capability-routes.test.ts` pin: trailing-comma `ELIZA_CAPABILITY_ROUTER_URLS` via `process.env` and via `config.env.vars`, a corrupt `ELIZA_CAPABILITY_ROUTER_ALLOWED_MODULES`, a corrupt `ELIZA_CAPABILITY_ROUTER_TRUST_POLICY`, a corrupt `ELIZA_CAPABILITY_ROUTER_TRUST_AUDIT`, a non-array endpoint list, and the `persist: false` path still connecting. Each corrupt case asserts the 500 names the setting, no persist call happened, the provider was never invoked, and the stored value is byte-identical afterwards. The suite's `afterEach` now also restores `ELIZA_CAPABILITY_ROUTER_TRUST_POLICY` / `_TRUST_AUDIT`, which earlier tests leak into `process.env` through the `persistConfigEnv` stub (that leak masked the trust-policy case).

## Why

`readPersistedEndpoints` parsed `ELIZA_CAPABILITY_ROUTER_URLS` inside a `catch` that returned `[]`; `persistEndpointInner` merged the new endpoint into that empty list and called `persistConfigEnv`/`saveConfig`, so every previously configured endpoint was overwritten and the route answered `success: true`. The module-allowlist and trust-audit readers (and the trust-policy reader) had the same shape, so a hand-edit typo or partial write was silently "fixed" by deleting the rest of the list, allowlist, or audit history on the next connect. Error-policy J3 requires an explicit invalid result, not a fake-valid default; failing before the connect keeps the runtime and the stored value consistent with what the client is told.

# Behavior

School source configuration, ingestion, and approved calendar application now reject new work after the family workspace deletion fence. Ingestion holds a durable source/run claim through PDF extraction and persisted review; application holds its claim through calendar delivery and the final database receipt.

A known fetch failure releases the claim after recording failure. A lost storage acknowledgement preserves the PDF hash and canonical media URL, and an unknown calendar outcome preserves the source/run/application identity. Deletion cannot race these unfinished operations. Claims have no time-based expiry.

# Risks

Low. This adds one versioned, additive WebSocket control frame only when a client supplies a valid cursor that the bounded buffer cannot satisfy. Connections without a cursor retain the historical 120-event tail, and ordinary event envelopes are unchanged. Cursor-aware clients should treat `replay-gap` as a request to refresh authoritative HTTP state.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Adds a shared `WebSocketReplayGap` wire contract with explicit gap reasons and available/replayed sequence ranges.
- Detects retention gaps, replay-limit truncation, unsequenced buffered events, and cursors from a later server sequence epoch.
- Sends the gap control frame before the recoverable event tail; a cursor from an earlier epoch receives the current epoch's bounded tail.
- Preserves the existing event-only helper for callers that rely on its historical selection behavior.
- Documents the reconnect cursor and required HTTP resync response.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking, additive protocol signal).

# Documentation changes needed?

Documentation is required and has been updated in `packages/docs/websocket-events.mdx`.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

Start with `packages/agent/src/api/server-ws-replay-gap.real-server.test.ts`. It starts the real API server, publishes 125 events through the authenticated HTTP route, reconnects a real WebSocket with `lastEventId=0`, and verifies that the gap frame precedes the bounded 120-event tail.

3d:Tbdc,Closes #31246

## What

- `plugins/plugin-meetings/src/actions/shared.ts`: adds `MEETING_URL_PARAMETER` (`meetingUrl`, aliases `url`/`meeting_url`/`link`) and `SESSION_ID_PARAMETER` (`sessionId`, aliases `session_id`/`session`), the two declarations the shared targeting helpers read.
- `join-meeting.ts`: declares `meetingUrl`, `botName` (aliases `bot_name`/`displayName`), and `language` (aliases `lang`/`locale`).
- `leave-meeting.ts`, `get-meeting-transcript.ts`: declare `sessionId` and `meetingUrl`, matching what `resolveTargetSession` reads.
- New `src/actions/planner-parameters.test.ts`: runs the real core `normalizeParamAliases` + `validateToolArgs` over each action, asserts `valid: true`, then delivers the validated bag through `options.parameters` and asserts the value reaches `requestJoin` / `stopSession` / the transcript lookup; undeclared arguments still reject.

## Why

Core's `validateToolArgs` rejects any argument an action does not declare, so with no `parameters` on these actions every planner call carrying `meetingUrl`, `botName`, `language`, or `sessionId` failed with `Unexpected argument` and was retried bare. `botName`/`language` were unreachable, and with two or more active meetings `LEAVE_MEETING` asked "which one should I leave?" but the planner could never answer with `sessionId`, so disambiguation dead-ended unless the user pasted the URL. The handlers already read the validated bag under `options.parameters` (`optionString` checks both levels), so the fix is the declarations plus aliases; core's pre-validation normalizer remaps `url` onto `meetingUrl` before the schema check.

## Evidence

```

+     "Unexpected argument 'meetingUrl'",
+     "Unexpected argument 'botName'",
+     "Unexpected argument 'language'",
 FAIL  JOIN_MEETING planner parameters > remaps the url alias onto meetingUrl
+     "Unexpected argument 'url'",
 FAIL  LEAVE_MEETING planner parameters > accepts sessionId and uses it to disambiguate several active meetings
+     "Unexpected argument 'sessionId'",
 FAIL  GET_MEETING_TRANSCRIPT planner parameters > accepts sessionId and reads that session's transcript, not the newest
+     "Unexpected argument 'sessionId'",

3e:T13bb,Closes #31249

## What

- `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/lifeops/meeting-ghost/index.ts`: `MeetingGhostTranscript` gains an optional IANA `timeZone`. `parseDueDate` projects the meeting instant into that zone with `getZonedDateParts` and walks days with `addDaysToLocalDate` / `getWeekdayForLocalDate` (from `lifeops/time.ts`); the 17:00 ledger `dueAt` and the 09:00 calendar deadline are wall times in that zone built with `buildUtcDateFromLocalParts`. An explicit `YYYY-MM-DD` is re-validated so an impossible day (e.g. `02-30`) yields no instant instead of rolling forward. The header documents the zone precedence: transcript `timeZone` -> owner's configured zone -> process zone.
- `consumer.ts`: `runMeetingGhostForTranscript` stamps a transcript that carries no zone with `resolveOwnerTimeZone(runtime, <meeting instant>)` before analysis, so the reachable event path (`event-handler.ts`, whose finalized payload carries no zone) resolves on the owner's calendar day.
- Existing fixtures in `test/meeting-ghost.test.ts` and `test/meeting-ghost.integration.test.ts` are pinned to `timeZone: "UTC"` so their instant assertions stay deterministic on any host.
- New `test/meeting-ghost.local-due-dates.test.ts` pins the four cases from the issue (with the transcript zone, and again through the process-zone fallback), an explicit date, and an impossible date.

## Why

`parseDueDate` resolved "tomorrow"/weekday phrases on the UTC calendar day of `meetingStartedAt` (`setUTCDate`, `getUTCDay`, `toISOString().slice(0,10)`), and `dueDateToLedgerDueAt` / the calendar deadline pinned `T17:00:00Z` / `T09:00:00Z`. A Monday 5:30pm PT meeting is already Tuesday in UTC, so "by tomorrow" landed on Wednesday and "by Friday" a week out; a Monday 8am JST meeting is still Sunday in UTC, so "by tomorrow" landed on Monday itself and "by Monday" on the meeting day. The ledger `dueAt`, follow-up approvals, and "Deadline:" calendar intent all carried that day. Resolving on the meeting's local day and building the instants in that zone fixes every consumer at once.

## Evidence

Red on `develop` (`5b183d21ff25`) with the new test only (`bunx vitest run test/meeting-ghost.local-due-dates.test.ts` in `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant`):

43:Tff3,Closes #31248

## What

- `plugins/plugin-health/src/actions/health.ts` and `screen-time.ts`: the runners take a required `resolveTimeZone(runtime)` adapter and build the "today" key with the existing `getZonedDateParts`/`getLocalDateKey` helpers (`normalizeTimeZone` validates the returned zone). `context.date`, `data.date`, the HealthKit `--date` request, and the connector-summary pick all use that key.
- `HealthActionService.getHealthTrend(days, { timeZone })` carries the zone through LifeOps (`service-mixin-health.ts`, `service.ts`, `domains/health-service.ts`) to a new `HealthBridgeConfig.timeZone`; `getRecentSummaries` walks local days with `addDaysToLocalDate`, and the fixture backend's offset-zero day follows the same zone. `todayDateKeyUtc()` is gone.
- `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/actions/health.ts` and `screen-time.ts` supply `resolveOwnerTimeZone(runtime, now)` (owner timezone fact, falling back to the process zone).
- Tests: `health.local-day.test.ts`, `screen-time.local-day.test.ts`, `health-bridge.local-day.test.ts` run the real runners / fixture bridge at `22:30Z` under `Asia/Tokyo`, `America/Los_Angeles`, `Pacific/Kiritimati`.

## Why

Every "today" in the health plugin came from `new Date().toISOString().slice(0, 10)`, the UTC calendar day. HealthKit's `--date` is a device-local day and screen-time / connector rows are keyed by local day, so a Tokyo owner asking before 09:00 JST (or a Kiritimati owner before 14:00) was handed yesterday's day and the reply labelled it as today; the N-day trend ended on yesterday as well. The runners now resolve the owner's zone once per invocation and every day key and the trend walk are computed in that zone.

## Evidence

       × walks the trend window in Pacific/Kiritimati
       ...

45:Teaf,# Relates to

Part of #31180 and #30123; neither issue is closed by this change.

# Behavior

Monthly packet creation, external draft creation, and draft revision now check the durable family workspace lifecycle in the same transaction as their write. Once deletion fences the workspace, these operations reject without adding private packet or draft content.

Enqueuing a reviewed draft holds a durable operation through canonical approval persistence and reminder surfacing. Deletion waits while that operation is in flight. Successful completion settles the claim; an uncertain acknowledgement retains the packet ID and draft version for reconciliation instead of permitting deletion to race unfinished work.

# Validation

The six new regression cases use fresh real PGlite databases. Four prove fenced packet, draft, revision, and approval mutations leave existing rows unchanged. Two exercise delayed reminder completion and a lost acknowledgement. The approval queue is an injected collaborator for this lifetime boundary; no provider message is sent.

Before repair, all four fenced mutation cases incorrectly succeeded. After repair, the complete packet integration suite passed 17 tests. The personal-assistant package suite passed 2,668 tests with six existing skips across 303 files. Repository verification passed 373 tasks. These results ran at the pre-rebase source tree; rebase onto merged #31226 preserved that tree byte for byte.

After rebase at `3c94b3da387aec0e75340459f0de182a321b5aae`, `bun install` made no changes and `bun run verify` passed 373/373 tasks. The exact-head database artifact producer passed all six selected cases. The other 11 packet tests were intentionally excluded from that capture run, not skipped by the package suite.

# Scope remaining

This is the packet mutation portion of the deletion workflow. It does not add a deletion screen, purge private data, revoke reads, select backup retention, or deploy the pilot. School writers and the complete owner deletion journey remain in #31180.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Reuses the existing chat gateway telemetry for all three embedding paths: direct pass-through, single SDK input and SDK batches. Responses report bounded auth-cache diagnostics and auth/middle/reserve/setup/pre-forward durations. This lets live probes distinguish a cache lookup or gateway admission delay from time after provider handoff.

## What kind of change is this?

Backend diagnostics for a measured Dedicated chat delay; no latency improvement is claimed from instrumentation alone.

# Documentation changes needed?

No user-facing documentation change. Uses the existing HTTP telemetry format.

4c:Tb06,Closes #31241

## What

- `POST /api/whatsapp/disconnect`: a failed `state.saveConfig()` now restores `connectors.whatsapp` in memory and answers a structured `500 { ok: false, error: "Failed to save connector config: <cause>" }` instead of `{ ok: true }`.
- Pairing `connected` handler: a failed persist restores the connector entry and the `agents.defaults.ownerContacts.whatsapp` entry (including any container objects the handler created), logs a structured error, and broadcasts a `whatsapp-status` event with `status: "error"` and the same message. The `connected` event fires after the pair response has already been sent, so the pairing status channel is the boundary that can still report it.
- Both catches are annotated `// error-policy:J1`.
- Three tests in `whatsapp-routes.auth-scope.test.ts` pin the disconnect 500 + rollback, the pairing rollback + error broadcast on a fresh config, and the pairing rollback of a pre-existing connector and owner contact.

## Why

Both handlers mutated `state.config` first and then wrapped `saveConfig()` in `catch { /* test envs */ }`. When the write failed the route reported success, but the on-disk config the connector loader reads on restart (`health-routes.ts`, `connector-health.ts`) still had the previous state, so a logged-out WhatsApp session was restarted, or a freshly paired account was missing. `packages/agent/src/api/connector-routes.ts` handles the same config object by rolling back and returning 500; this change follows that model.

## Evidence

Red on `origin/develop` (`5b183d21ff25`) with the new tests:

```
× disconnect answers 500 and restores the in-memory connector when saveConfig throws
  AssertionError: expected 200 to be 500 // Object.is equality
× pairing connected rolls back the connector and owner contact and broadcasts an error when saveConfig throws
  AssertionError: expected { whatsapp: { …(2) } } to be undefined
× pairing connected keeps a pre-existing connector and owner contact when saveConfig throws
  AssertionError: expected { whatsapp: { enabled: true, …(2) } } to deeply equal { whatsapp: { enabled: false, …(1) } }
Tests  3 failed | 19 passed (22)
```

58:Ta57,Closes #31243

## What

- `plugins/plugin-discord/messages.ts`: `DISCORD_ENVELOPE_ENABLED` and `DISCORD_DRAFT_STREAMING` are resolved through core `parseBooleanValue` (exported from `@elizaos/core` via `utils/boolean`) instead of string-literal comparisons, keeping the previous defaults (`envelopeEnabled` on, `draftStreamingEnabled` off) and the `"1"`/`"0"` spellings.
- `__tests__/messages-boolean-settings.test.ts`: constructs `MessageManager` with a runtime whose `getSetting` is a real `AgentRuntime`'s, asserts the runtime hands the manager boolean `true`/`false`, and pins the resolved flags for the coerced booleans, unset, `"1"`/`"0"`, and an unparseable value.
- No `@elizaos/core` test mock needed updating: the two fixed-object mocks in the plugin (`discord-local-resubscribe`, `discord-local-service-stale-socket`) load `discord-local-service.ts`, not `messages.ts`; the others spread `importOriginal()`. The full plugin suite passes.

## Why

`AgentRuntime.getSetting` (`packages/core/src/runtime.ts`) returns boolean `true`/`false` for the strings `"true"`/`"false"`, so `getSetting("DISCORD_DRAFT_STREAMING") === "true"` was always false and `getSetting("DISCORD_ENVELOPE_ENABLED") !== "false"` was always true. An operator setting `DISCORD_DRAFT_STREAMING=true` got no draft streaming and one setting `DISCORD_ENVELOPE_ENABLED=false` still got envelopes, silently. The existing component-delivery test only passed because its fake runtime returned raw strings.

## Evidence

Red on `origin/develop` (`5b183d21ff25`) with the new test (the real-runtime coercion check itself passes; the manager assertion fails):

```
× enables draft streaming and disables envelopes from the coerced booleans
  AssertionError: expected { draftStreamingEnabled: false, …(1) } to deeply equal { draftStreamingEnabled: true, …(1) }
  -   "draftStreamingEnabled": true,
  -   "envelopeEnabled": false,
  +   "draftStreamingEnabled": false,
  +   "envelopeEnabled": true,
Tests  1 failed | 4 passed (5)
```

Green after the fix (`bunx vitest run` in `plugins/plugin-discord`):

```
Test Files  100 passed (100)
     Tests  870 passed (870)
```

- `bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-discord typecheck` → `tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` exits 0 with no diagnostics.

59:Tbfe,Closes #31247

## What

- `plugins/plugin-local-inference/src/actions/identify-speaker.ts`: declares `name` and `profileId` (aliases `profile_id`, `voiceProfileId`) as `parameters`; `optionString` now reads `options.<key>` first (existing direct callers) and then `options.parameters.<key>` (the validated bag core delivers on the planner path). Header updated to state the contract.
- `plugins/plugin-local-inference/__tests__/identify-speaker.test.ts`: new `planner parameters` cases running the real core `validateToolArgs` over the action, asserting `valid: true` for `{ name, profileId }`, that an undeclared argument still rejects, and that the validated bag delivered under `options.parameters` reaches the handler with the explicit `profileId` winning over a more recently observed unbound profile. The existing top-level-options case stays as the backward-compatibility check.

## Why

Core's `validateToolArgs` rejects any argument an action does not declare, so with no `parameters` on `IDENTIFY_SPEAKER` a planner call carrying `name` or `profileId` failed with `Unexpected argument` before the handler ran. Independently, the handler's `optionString` read only top-level `options[key]`, while core delivers validated planner arguments nested under `options.parameters`, so even a declared value would not have been read. Together these made the header's "an explicit `profileId` option wins" unreachable from the planner and left the name to regex extraction over the message text. The fix declares the two parameters and reads the planner bag, keeping the top-level read for direct callers.

## Evidence

Red on develop `5b183d21ff25` (`NODE_OPTIONS='--experimental-sqlite' bunx vitest run __tests__/identify-speaker.test.ts` in `plugins/plugin-local-inference`):

```

+     "Unexpected argument 'name'",
+     "Unexpected argument 'profileId'",
+   ],
+   "valid": false,
 FAIL  identifySpeakerAction planner parameters > still rejects an undeclared argument
+   "Unexpected argument 'name'",
    "Unexpected argument 'entityId'",
 FAIL  identifySpeakerAction planner parameters > reads name and an explicit profileId from the validated planner bag, and the explicit id wins

## What

- `plugins/plugin-registry/src/api/app-plugins-routes.ts`: the inner `vault://KEY` fallback lookup in `POST /api/plugins/:id/reveal` now distinguishes a `VaultMissError` (still `200 { ok: true, value: null }`) from any other vault failure, which logs a warning and answers the same `500 "Vault reveal failed"` the outer lookup already returns. The catch is annotated `// error-policy:J1`.
- `app-plugins-routes.test.ts`: the `sharedVault` mock now exposes `reveal`/`get` spies and the `isVaultRef`/`parseVaultRef` mocks follow the real `vault://` semantics; three tests cover the inner lookup throwing a keychain error (500), missing (`value: null`), and resolving (the inner value).

## Why

The outer `sharedVault().reveal` already treats only `VaultMissError` as a miss and surfaces every other failure as a 500. When the env/config fallback is a `vault://` sentinel, the second `sharedVault().get(innerKey)` was wrapped in `catch { /* fall through to null */ }`, so a locked or unavailable keychain produced `value: null`. The plugin config UI then showed a secret that exists as unset and prompted the user to re-enter it, with the same failure getting two different answers depending on which lookup hit it.

5d:Tc47,Closes #31239

## What

`PUT /api/permissions/trade-mode` in `packages/agent/src/api/permissions-routes-extra.ts` now captures the previous `features` object and `tradePermissionMode` before writing. If `ctx.saveElizaConfig` throws, the handler restores exactly the prior in-memory state (removes a `features` object it created, removes a key that did not exist, or puts the old value back), logs at error level, and answers a structured 500 `Failed to save trade permission mode: <cause>` (error-policy J1) instead of `ok: true`. Modeled on the `POST /api/connectors` rollback in `connector-routes.ts`.

Tests: `permissions-routes-extra.mode-persistence.test.ts` gains two failing-write cases (an existing `agent-auto` mode stays `agent-auto` with sibling keys intact; a first-ever write leaves `features` undefined so the resolver falls back to `user-sign-only`). The dispatcher suite's "returns the updated mode when persistence throws" test pinned the old fabricated success and is rewritten to assert the 500, the rollback, and that `json` is never called.

## Why

The handler mutated `state.config.features.tradePermissionMode` in memory, wrapped the disk write in a catch that only `logger.warn`ed, and unconditionally answered 200 with the new mode. The in-memory value lived until restart, when the on-disk value returned, so an owner who switched **off** `agent-auto` trading saw success and the agent was auto-trading again after the next restart with no indication anything failed. Repository policy is that a failed write must not be reported as success and the in-memory state must not diverge from what was persisted.

## Evidence

Red on develop `5b183d21ff25` (`bunx vitest run src/api/permissions-routes-extra.mode-persistence.test.ts -t "persistence failure"` in `packages/agent`):

```

 FAIL  ... > rolls the trade mode back and answers 500 when the config write fails

      Tests  2 failed | 11 skipped (13)
```

Green after the fix (`bunx vitest run src/api/permissions-routes-extra.mode-persistence.test.ts src/api/permissions-routes-extra.test.ts`):

5e:Tb48,Closes #31244

## What

- `plugins/plugin-elizacloud/src/services/cloud-auth.ts`: the device auto-signup gate is now `isTruthyCloudFlag(getSetting(this.runtime, "ELIZAOS_CLOUD_ENABLED"))`, using the plugin's stringifying `getSetting` and the existing truthiness helper, so boolean `true`, `"true"` and `"1"` all enable auto-signup and `"false"`, `"0"` and unset keep it off.
- `plugins/plugin-elizacloud/src/utils/config.ts`: `isTruthyCloudFlag` is exported; its doc now says why callers must read the flag through the stringifying `getSetting`.
- `__tests__/cloud-auth-device-signup.test.ts`: starts `CloudAuthService` against a real `AgentRuntime` whose secrets set `ELIZAOS_CLOUD_ENABLED`, asserts the runtime hands back boolean `true`, spies `authenticateWithDevice`, and pins `"true"`, `"1"` (invoked) and `"false"`, `"0"`, unset (not invoked).

## Why

Core `AgentRuntime.getSetting` coerces `"true"` to boolean `true` before returning, and the plugin manifest declares `ELIZAOS_CLOUD_ENABLED` as a boolean parameter, so `enabled === "true"` never matched and only the literal `"1"` reached `authenticateWithDevice`. With no API key the service logged `[CloudAuth] Cloud not enabled (set ELIZAOS_CLOUD_ENABLED=true)`, the instruction the operator had already followed. The rest of the plugin already reads cloud flags through `isTruthyCloudFlag`; this path bypassed it.

## Evidence

5f:Te06,Closes #31238

## What

`packages/agent/src/api/wallet-evm-balance.ts` gains one helper, `requireJsonRpcResult`, that rejects any keyed-provider body whose parsed envelope carries an `error` member (JSON-RPC object, or the bare string Alchemy's NFT REST API can emit). It throws a typed `ElizaError` with code `EVM_PROVIDER_RPC_ERROR`, context `{ provider, chain, chainId, rpcCode }`, and the provider's own message. The helper wraps the Alchemy `eth_getBalance` and `alchemy_getTokenBalances` calls, the Ankr `ankr_getAccountBalance` call, and both NFT calls (`getNFTsForOwner`, `ankr_getNFTsByOwner`). The public-RPC path keeps its existing `data.error?.message` check unchanged.

Five new tests in `wallet-evm-balance.test.ts` pin the 200-with-error envelope on each path: balances assert `error !== null` carrying the provider message with no zero balances presented as healthy; the NFT paths assert the envelope is rejected and logged with the chain and provider message rather than silently read as an empty collection (`WalletEvmNftCollection` has no error field, so the existing per-chain NFT boundary is kept as-is; widening that public DTO is out of scope here).

## Why

Alchemy and Ankr answer HTTP 200 with `{"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":1,"error":{...}}` for compute-unit throttling, invalid keys, and unsupported chains. `jsonOrThrow` only rejected non-2xx or non-JSON bodies, and the keyed paths read a missing `result` as `0n` / `[]`, so every throttled chain was emitted as `nativeBalance: "0", tokens: [], error: null` and `makeEvmChainFailure` never fired. The wallet UI then showed $0 on every EVM chain, and `wallet-balance-delta` (which excludes only chains with `error !== null`) took the false zero as a real sample and produced spurious "dropped to zero" / "restored" notifications. With the helper the throw reaches the existing per-chain `catch` in `fetchEvmBalances`, which reports it through `makeEvmChainFailure` with the provider text.

## Evidence

Red on develop `5b183d21ff25` (`bunx vitest run src/api/wallet-evm-balance.test.ts -t "JSON-RPC error envelopes"` in `packages/agent`):

```
     × reports an Alchemy native-balance error envelope as a chain failure 25ms
     × reports an Alchemy token-balance error envelope even when the native call succeeded 6ms
     × reports an Ankr account-balance error envelope as a chain failure 6ms
     × rejects an Alchemy NFT error envelope instead of reading it as an empty collection 4ms
     × rejects an Ankr NFT error envelope instead of reading it as an empty collection 1ms
 FAIL  ... > reports an Alchemy native-balance error envelope as a chain failure
AssertionError: expected null not to be null
      Tests  5 failed | 32 skipped (37)
```

Green after the fix (`bunx vitest run src/api/wallet-evm-balance.test.ts src/api/wallet-evm-balance.surrogate.test.ts`):

```
 Test Files  2 passed (2)
      Tests  41 passed (41)
```

Lint: `bunx biome check packages/agent/src/api/wallet-evm-balance.ts packages/agent/src/api/wallet-evm-balance.test.ts` → `Checked 2 files in 64ms. No fixes applied.`

## What

Move plugin-computeruse's OCR registry state (the provider map plus the coord-OCR and Set-of-Marks slots in `src/mobile/ocr-provider.ts`) from module scope to a `globalThis` slot keyed by `Symbol.for("elizaos.plugin-computeruse.ocr-provider-registry")`, read on every access with no module-local cache. Public functions and signatures are unchanged.

## Why

The package bundles this module into both `dist/index.js` and the `mobile/ocr-provider` subpath. plugin-vision registers through the subpath (`plugins/plugin-vision/src/index.ts:118-141`), while `runOcrOrDetect`, the scene OCR adapter and the app-control defaults read from the index bundle, so packaged builds always saw an empty registry: `ocr` and `detect_elements` failed with "No OCR provider is registered" even with plugin-vision loaded, and `scene.ocr` stayed empty. Dev scripts pass `--conditions=eliza-source`, which resolves both imports to the same source file and masked it. This is the same dual-bundle hazard core's `ambient-context.ts` documents, and the fix uses the same `globalThis[Symbol.for]` pattern (`packages/core/src/boot-env.ts`).

# Background

## What does this PR do?

The Electrobun red-green control removes the `@elizaos/capacitor-bun-runtime` path mapping and expects the audit to report it unresolved, but it also pinned the reported *importer* to `packages/app-core/src/platform/ios-runtime-bridge.ts`. The audit reports the first importer it meets, which is now `packages/ui/src/api/ios-local-agent-transport.ts`; the same pin was already re-pointed once in #30709. The assertion now matches any workspace importer, because the contract the control proves is that the missing mapping is detected, not which file imports the package first.

## What kind of change is this?

Test repair for a suite that is red at the `develop` tip (see the issue for the failing lanes in develop validation run 34694307489).

## What

In `.github/workflows/test.yml`, run the app-core screenshot-quality diagnostics file directly:

```
bunx vitest run --config packages/app-core/vitest.config.ts packages/app-core/test/app/screenshot-quality.test.ts
```

instead of `bun run --cwd packages/app-core test -- test/app/screenshot-quality.test.ts`, matching the neighbouring app step.

## Why

The app-core `test` script is a `&&` chain, so Bun appends the file path to its last command (`bun run test:script-suites test/app/screenshot-quality.test.ts`) and vitest runs the whole suite. That suite now includes `steward-sidecar-login.test.ts`, which needs the `@elizaos/login` workspace the diagnostics job never builds, so `tests / Zero-Key diagnostics` fails on a test the step was never meant to run (latest: [run 34723784763](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/34723784763/job/103635168222): `1 failed | 365 passed`). 78:T733,## Observed failure

Hosted evidence: https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/34739463910/job/103676715827

I reproduced the same failure locally with pinned Bun 1.3.14 and Chromium. Opening the generated fixture with the runner's en-US locale, UTC timezone, fixed clock, and existing initialization script gives:

```json
{"errors":["TypeError: client2.onAuthorityChange is not a function"],"clockCount":0,"body":""}
```

The inline API-client stub in `packages/ui/src/components/shell/__e2e__/run-home-locale-e2e.mjs` exposes only `getBaseUrl` and `fetch`. The mounted real widgets now reach the client authority subscription contract through auth/runtime state. The separate home-screen fixture was fixed in #31199; the locale runner owns a different stub and remains broken.

## Acceptance

- The locale fixture implements the current stable-client subscription contract without changing or weakening production authorization.
- `bun run --cwd packages/ui test:home-locale-e2e` passes for en-US and de-DE, preserving the fixed-clock hour-cycle and Fahrenheit/Celsius assertions against the rendered real widgets.
- Page errors are surfaced at their cause rather than hidden behind a readiness timeout; missing weather must not be reported as verified temperature behavior.
- Inspect the locale captures, run owning and repository checks, and verify the corresponding hosted step on the merged revision.

This is a reproduced browser-harness defect encountered during Bettina's final acceptance work (#30123), not a coverage inventory request. It is separate from the retired-header expectations in #31214.
79:T843,## Summary

`tests / Script Tests (Linux)` in the develop "Validate develop" workflow has been red since 2026-09-11. One of its causes is `packages/scripts/__tests__/staging-cloud-receipt.test.ts`: two cases fail before any receipt assertion runs.

```
error: [cloud-live-continuity] artifact must use the exact closed schema
      at requireClosedRecord (packages/app/test/cloud-live-continuity-contract.ts:2134:5)
      at parseCloudLiveContinuityEvidence (packages/app/test/cloud-live-continuity-contract.ts:2379:20)
      at createDeployedRendererProof (packages/cloud/scripts/pages-deployment-authority.mjs:836:22)
      at deployedProofFile (packages/scripts/__tests__/staging-cloud-receipt.test.ts:72:17)
(fail) staging Cloud live receipt > persists closed correlation in receipt v4 from a validated proof file
(fail) staging Cloud live receipt > rejects boolean claims and proof/source/latency mismatches
```

Latest red run: https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/34723784763/job/103635168326 (develop `1fa9dbf22d74`).

## Root cause

`7e9e3f946a0e` ("fix(cloud): validate deployed continuity with the browser contract") made `createDeployedRendererProof` parse its `continuity` input with `parseCloudLiveContinuityEvidence`, which requires the closed `schemaVersion: 2` record including the Dedicated mutation keys. The receipt test still builds the proof with the retired `schemaVersion: 1` literal (`reloadHistoryPassed`, `freshContextHistoryPassed`, no `dedicated*` keys), so the fixture itself is rejected. The sibling suite `packages/cloud/scripts/__tests__/pages-deployment-authority.test.ts` was updated in that commit to build the record with `createCloudLiveContinuityEvidence`; this test was not.

## Acceptance criteria

- `bun test packages/scripts/__tests__/staging-cloud-receipt.test.ts` passes (8/8) on develop.
- The proof fixture is produced by the continuity contract's own builder rather than a hand-copied literal, so future key additions cannot silently drift.
- The "does not match the receipt source/run identity" and "latency does not match" rejections are still exercised.
7a:T99b,A successful owner upload currently reaches a dead end before agreement review. On deployed revision917f68d490923502a866c7800e56eb2b6493578a, a two-page synthetic agreement was saved and exported correctly, but the owner read returns `obligations: []`. FamilyOperationsView only renders/approves/rejects existing proposals. OWNER_AGREEMENT_KNOWLEDGE supports list/read/approve/reject/pin/unpin/preview, and no proposal-creation operation. The only production caller of AgreementKnowledgeService.proposeObligation is the raw POST obligations route.

Affected user: an owner uploads a PDF and expects the application/agent to help review its cited requirements before making them active. Today an operator must invent a backend API call to populate the review list; a successful upload is not a usable review journey.

Evidence: [deployed owner upload, empty owner obligation list, immutable export and reviewed extraction](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/releases/download/pr-evidence-12/family-917f68d4-owner-pdf.zip). This was a synthetic fixture; no real co-parent was contacted. Related draft #31032 and extraction issue #31026.

Acceptance:

- Provide an owner-accessible review entry point in the agreement UI and an equivalent discoverable owner agent action. The ordinary user must not supply backend identifiers or manually construct citation payloads.
- Generate reviewable proposals from complete persisted page evidence, preserving source version and page citations. Treat document instructions as source data; do not fabricate missing terms, resolve conflicts, or turn silence into consent.
- Validate proposed citation/page bindings against the saved source. Show explicit no-proposals, failed and incomplete outcomes instead of a healthy-looking empty review list.
- Keep proposal generation separate from approval, pinning, sharing and scheduling. Only exact owner-approved proposals become active; preview/generation must not send messages or create calendar events.
- Retry/lost-response/restart behavior must not duplicate proposals or overwrite prior owner decisions. Preserve immutable original bytes and extraction history.
- Verify the real owner upload -> cited proposal generation -> approve/reject -> pinned-context retrieval flow with a live model and controlled synthetic data. Include desktop/phone interaction, source/output trajectory, persistence and failure/idempotency evidence; mocked proposal lists alone are insufficient.
7b:T5cf,## Reproduced failure

On the Bettina HTTPS pilot at `58cd2df949d37627131de2427ca043e218385e09`, a normal owner password login succeeds and authenticated `GET /api/status` returns 200 with `canRespond: true`. A same-origin browser WebSocket using that session closes with code 1008, `Unauthorized`, after a ping instead of receiving a pong. The backend recorded 36 pre-auth message rejections during a fresh 50-second observation.

The app-core WebSocket authorizer checks query/header session tokens but omits the first-party browser session cookie. REST and WebSocket authentication consequently disagree for a normal browser login.

## Acceptance

- Admit a current owner browser session through the canonical session store and role resolution, retaining the existing origin boundary.
- Reject expired, revoked, malformed, unknown and non-owner cookie sessions; do not introduce a trusted-loopback or missing-origin shortcut for cookie admission.
- Preserve existing bearer/device authentication behavior and fail closed on store errors.
- Exercise the real HTTP/WebSocket boundary and session storage, run package/root checks and exact-head hosted checks, and repeat password-login plus same-origin ping/pong on the deployed pilot. Keep credentials out of evidence.

Part of Bettina deployment acceptance in #30123. This is separate from the model action-discovery repair #31181 and from the cloud/native session-authority work in #30920, which does not change this host authorizer.

88:T8b6,## Summary

The auth audit log writer `appendJsonLine` (`packages/app-core/src/api/auth/audit.ts:115-121`) does `fs.appendFile(filePath, JSON.stringify(line) + "\n")` into `<state>/auth/audit.log`, the file the header documents as the operator's history "even if pglite is wiped". This is the same shape #30878 describes; #30879 adds `appendJsonlRecord` to `@elizaos/shared` but fixes only `capability-broker.ts`, `account-pool-consumer-metering.ts` and `account-usage.ts`.

```

The second real event (`token_revoke`) is inside the unparseable line and invisible to `jq -c`, `grep`, or the rotate-then-read workflow the header describes.

Meeting-ghost relative due dates are resolved on the UTC calendar day of the meeting. `parseDueDate` in `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/lifeops/meeting-ghost/index.ts:355-370` does `new Date(meetingStartedAt)`, adds days with `setUTCDate`, reads the weekday with `getUTCDay()` and formats with `toISOString().slice(0,10)`; `dueDateToLedgerDueAt` (373-377) then pins `${dueDate}T17:00:00.000Z` and the calendar deadline (436) `T09:00:00.000Z`. `event-handler.ts:67` feeds every transcript through this path.

## Reproduction (develop `5b183d21ff25`)

Probe calling the exported `analyzeMeetingGhostTranscript` (results are offset-driven, identical under any process TZ):

```

startedAt=2026-09-17T17:30:00-07:00 "Bob will send the deck by Friday"       -> dueDate=2026-09-25  (Thu 5:30pm PT; Friday is 09-18)
startedAt=2026-09-14T08:00:00+09:00 "Alice will send the report by tomorrow" -> dueDate=2026-09-14  (Mon 8am JST; tomorrow is 09-15)

- Relative dates are resolved on the meeting's local calendar day (the transcript's or runtime's timezone), and the ledger/calendar instants are built from that zone.
- Tests pin the four cases above.

8d:T68b,## Summary

`packages/cloud/services/gateway-discord/src/server-router.ts:26` and `packages/cloud/services/gateway-webhook/src/server-router.ts:21` compute `Number(process.env.KEDA_COOLDOWN_SECONDS ?? 900)`. `??` guards only an unset variable, so any present-but-invalid value yields `NaN`, which flows into `redis.expire(key, NaN)` in `_common/src/gateway-routing.ts:40`. The `@upstash/redis` client drops the `NaN` argument and sends a one-argument `EXPIRE`, which Redis rejects.

## Reproduction (develop `5b183d21ff25`)

`bun --conditions=eliza-source test` probe in gateway-discord with `KEDA_COOLDOWN_SECONDS=abc`, fetch overridden to capture the Upstash pipeline body:

```
PROBE bodies: [["lpush","keda:srv-1:activity","…"]],[["ltrim","keda:srv-1:activity",0,0]],[["expire","keda:srv-1:activity"]]
PROBE thrown: Command failed: ERR value is not an integer or out of range
```

## Consequence

In gateway-discord the rejection lands in the `catch` at `gateway-manager.ts:1650` that also wraps `forwardToServer`, so every inbound message is counted as failed and never forwarded (`Failed to route message to server`). gateway-webhook only warns, but `lpush`/`ltrim` already ran, so the activity key persists with no TTL and KEDA never scales the server down. #30544 fixed the `parseInt(env || d)` shape in cloud/shared and states `Number(...)` is safe; that holds for `Number(x) || d`, not `Number(x ?? d)`.

## Acceptance criteria

- An invalid `KEDA_COOLDOWN_SECONDS` fails fast at boot with a typed error naming the variable (or falls back to the default with a warning; pick one and document it), never `NaN`.
- Tests pin the invalid-value case for both routers.

The plugin manifest (`package.json:348`) declares the key `"type": "boolean"`, and core `getSetting` coerces `"true"` to boolean `true` anyway, so only the literal `"1"` ever works. The plugin already has a correct helper, `isTruthyCloudFlag` in `src/utils/config.ts`, which stringifies first; this path bypasses it.

`packages/agent/src/api/wallet-evm-balance.ts` turns an Alchemy or Ankr JSON-RPC **error envelope** into a healthy, empty chain. Both providers answer HTTP 200 with `{"jsonrpc":"2.0","id":1,"error":{...}}` for compute-unit throttling, invalid keys, or unsupported chains. `jsonOrThrow` (line 172) only rejects non-2xx or non-JSON bodies, and the keyed paths never read the `error` member: `fetchAlchemyChainBalances` (385-421: `nativeData.result ? BigInt(...) : 0n`, `tokenData.result?.tokenBalances ?? []`), `fetchAnkrChainBalances` (511-515: `data.result?.assets ?? []`) and the NFT twins (801, 845). The public-RPC path (`fetchEvmNativeBalanceViaRpc`, 602) does check `data.error?.message`.

 {"chain":"Base",...,"error":null}, … {"chain":"Avalanche",...,"error":null}]
```

All seven chains, no `error` set.

## Consequence

Same DTO rule the repo guide states: a failed load must not look like a healthy zero. Related but different sites: #31110/#31111 (plugin-wallet's own providers).

## Root cause

`run-frontend-hosting-e2e.mjs` compiles the real Tailwind theme (`styles/base.css` + `styles/tailwind-theme.css`) and reads the computed background of `[data-testid="hosting-publish"]`, a default-variant `Button` (`bg-accent … hover:bg-accent-hover`). Since `d274fc06b832` ("fix(ui): unify grey surfaces and simplify view presentation", 2026-09-05, part of the same design pass as #31214) `base.css` defines the light-theme accent as black:

--accent-rgb: 0, 0, 0;
--accent-hover: #262626;
```

so the button renders `rgb(0,0,0)` at rest and `rgb(38,38,38)` on hover, exactly what the runner reports. The orange accent survives only in `styles/brand-gold.css` (`--accent: var(--classic-gold)`), which this e2e does not load. The repository guide still states the visual contract the e2e enforces (`CLAUDE.md`: "Orange is the accent; do not introduce blue, and use darker orange—not black—for an orange resting control's hover state").

Either the black accent is the intended brand and the guide plus the e2e's orange assertions (and any sibling fixture checks that pin "no black hover") move to the new tokens, or `--accent` in `base.css` should again resolve to the orange/gold accent. `scenarios / Zero-Key app browser core` in the develop "Validate develop" workflow has been red on every run since `619545c5357f` (PR #30989; first red run 2026-09-11 01:36Z, last green `77041c39df`).
